Faced with this new paradigm, startups are vehicles of innovation, attraction of capital, job creation and a great alternative for traditional professional outings. That is why it was more than necessary to recognise the specificity of emerging companies as an entrepreneurial initiative, projects that are sometimes born outside the system and thanks to the savings of the founders themselves who have enormous growth potential and that, if they validate their business model, they work, while at other times they remain on the road. Precisely because of this potential for growth, these innovation projects, by their nature and global audience, can grow in any other global capital, which is why it is more important than ever to offer the facilities and incentives needed to attract talent, investment and entrepreneurs to our country, especially in a sector such as startups, where its own process implies constant proof of error. This concludes the need for Spain to recognise a specific regulatory framework for startups, with the strategic objective of placing our country at the forefront of digital and technological entrepreneurship, always observing the best practices of these ecosystems and adapting to the Spanish economy. And we believe that this law to promote the ecosystem of emerging companies can finally begin to lay the foundations for the flowering of startups in our country. Now, is this the law that Citizens would have done if they had been ruling? Is that a perfect law? It is not, any law is always likely to improve. It leaves some loose ends and sins in some aspects of a certain conformism, but, ladies and gentlemen, it is a good law, above all, because for once - and we hope it will serve as a precedent - there has been a willingness and effort on the part of the Government to generate consensus. Many amendments have been accepted from all or almost all the parliamentary groups, and that is a good signal sent abroad that there is almost unanimous will in this House for this law to be a success. In the specific case of my parliamentary group, Citizens have managed to include very positive changes. During the course of this Act, we have succeeded in having eighteen of our amendments, either in their terms or in their transactions, accepted. In addition to important technical corrections, I would like to highlight some of the improvements achieved and accepted by the Government. Thus, we have extended the turnover limit from EUR 5 million to EUR 10 million, we have given Enisa Tools of collaboration with the private sector in processing to avoid the risk of collapse of the accreditation system of the innovative character of companies, we have expanded the benefits to serial entrepreneurs and we have improved the controlled testing environments, increasing their transparency and giving more information to both those involved in them and to society as a whole to learn from the experiences of other companies; in short, to create once and for all a true innovative ecosystem in which synergies are created for all participants. ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u6	Thank you, Madam President. We recognize positive elements in this law. We share the need to take steps to promote and facilitate the development of innovative enterprises, but, in our view, it is not a centralizing approach to this issue. As is well known, Galicia has exclusive competence in the promotion and planning of economic activity, as well as in the field of industry, and therefore this law, which aims to encourage the creation and relocation of emerging enterprises, cannot be designed from a centralist and uniformizing perspective. Our amendments have been aimed at alleviating and correcting this situation, trying to ensure that the standard respects the policy-making capacity of the autonomous communities in this area, but unfortunately the majority have not yet been taken into consideration. Why propose a single accreditation body at state level? Why should we also centralise the information points in this area? Why ignore the richness that the linguistic and cultural diversity of the various nations of the State can entail for innovation? Why not declare an activity as entrepreneurial when it has a particular interest in the scope of an autonomous community, and not only for the whole State? In our view, these issues have not been adequately resolved. We insist that the promotion of economic activity is indeed essential and that job creation should be facilitated and promoted from the public sphere, but it is not necessary to laminate and ignore the role of each country or autonomous community in this area. Despite the incorporation of some proposals, as I say, we believe that this has not been properly resolved, and that is why we maintain our amendments, we call for them to be supported and we hope that they will be adopted, because, of course, we are not going to vote against a law that we recognise positive elements, but we also believe that if these issues are not properly resolved we will not be able to vote in favour either. This is mainly due to the method used by the Government, vetoing 57 of the 271 amendments tabled, using a totalitarian approach and without any legal basis. Firstly, because amendments affecting the decrease in income and a generic concept are being vetoed without determining exactly what heading such an amendment would be concerned with. And, in addition, it is being done on the future benefit, when it should be the current benefit, because the decrease in budgetary revenue is not a fact that is likely to be certain, but a fact of probability. Secondly, because there is a distortion of the very raison d'être of the power of the veto of the Government recognized in article 134.6 of the Spanish Constitution, since the aforementioned power of the Government, is exercised as it is exercised in the proceedings, has to be examined by the Chamber. And there is lewdness on the part of the groups in accepting the vetoes, undermining the legislative powers of the Congress, so that this recently executed practice by the Government is preventing the parliamentary groups from carrying out their functions, as has happened in the paper and in the Commission. And, thirdly, because there is no precedent in the history of this House for vetoing amendments that affect the decline in income, when it is also an estimate. As the Government has raised an additional 28 billion up to September by breaking its forecasts, there is no point in vetoing amendments that affect a tax such as, for example, the IRPF, because, because of the elasticity of this tax, the application of a deduction in the IRPF may result in higher collection and higher revenues. This practice must be denounced as another element of this Government which manages the institutions at its own discretion. Ladies and gentlemen, tell me, what is the point, then, of parliamentary groups submitting amendments? Isn't it logical with a startup law to introduce amendments that affect tax laws? And if they do, isn't it logical that they can lower income? Also, on the contrary, it may happen that a deduction, for example in IRPF, increases them. Do you, ladies and gentlemen, agree to apply a practice that can be used by future governments? I have already told you that this parliamentary group will initiate the necessary legal measures to determine whether or not the veto on the amendments is in line with the law. Ladies and gentlemen, it turns out that some of the vetoed amendments proposed an ostensible improvement of the law, including increasing from five to seven - or from seven to twelve, if they are biotechnological - the years to be considered startups; increasing the base of deduction in IRPF for investment in startups from EUR 100 000 to EUR 1 million; increasing the limit of own funds to be considered startups from EUR 400 000 to EUR 1 million; increasing, for example, the three to five-year period for the application of the 15% tax rate on company tax on startups, or reducing the tax on certain digital services from 3 to 1.5% to taxpayers in Ceuta and Melilla. In total, the budgetary impact of the above-mentioned amendments which the Government vetoed amounts to EUR 128 million, i.e. almost EUR 130 million, the same amount that Mr Sánchez has promised to give to the Bill and Melinda Gates Foundation behind the back of the Spanish and less than the more than EUR 150 million that has been reserved for the Presidency of Europe. Ladies and gentlemen, it is an unprecedented act of hypocrisy and phariseeism by a government that vetoes illegal amendments, amendments that could have improved this law for entrepreneurs, SMEs, self-employed people and investors. For Sánchez's wishes there is no money to help them, but there is money for his whims and delusions of power when it comes to promoting himself and who knows if perhaps also preparing for his departure. Ladies and gentlemen, when we govern, we will repeal all ideological laws on the first day and return to the Spaniards the place they deserve and should never lose. Thank you so much. ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u16	Vice-President, ladies and gentlemen, a city like Palma, conditioned by the insular fact and by the dependence of tourism, needs this law of startups to boost a system of innovation of its own and stimulate the diversification of the economy. We are talking about one of the main standards developed by the Government for the promotion of new high-value-added economic activities that generate quality employment. It will undoubtedly have a positive impact on investment in emerging companies, on the development of R & D & i and on the consolidation of innovative companies. It is a key piece to boost innovation and the dynamism of the country's economy and to promote the development of new sustainable, resilient and high-quality jobs. In fact, this law is part of component 13 of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan and is complemented by the recently approved reform of the Competition Law and the Law on Business Creation and Growth. It should be noted that this is a law involving civil society actors and parliamentary groups. For this reason, we congratulate both the competent department, the Secretariat of State for Digitization and Artificial Intelligence, and all parliamentary groups on the ability to listen and the willingness to agree that have made it possible to improve the text during its parliamentary process. Our parliamentary group has worked on this law taking into account two issues whose results have been satisfactory to us. Firstly, to ensure that the impetus of startups and steps is not used solely and exclusively through tax benefits, thus preventing the State box from decreasing and generating competitive advantages over the rest of the companies. And it is that our tax system sins of lack of justice and progressiveness as far as corporation tax is concerned. Let's see an example. According to recent data from the Tax Agency on consolidated corporate tax accounts, large companies with a turnover of more than EUR 1 billion pay only 5% of their corporate tax profits, while companies with a turnover of between 1 and 6 million take on an effective rate of more than 18%, i.e. three times more. It is something that we cannot afford today if we want to build a solid welfare state, a just and progressive tax system and a business climate that guarantees competition and the generation of new opportunities. In this regard, we believe that this bill has succeeded in striking a balance between the various demands made by parliamentary agents and groups. The second issue on which my parliamentary group has worked has been to ensure that an innovative project can be set up as a cooperative or a working society, as we intend to accommodate and push this type of entrepreneurial activity, where economic interests are integrated with those of a social nature. In this way, a firm step is taken to comply with one of the requirements set out in the Constitution in Article 129.2: 'The public authorities shall effectively promote the various forms of participation in the enterprise and shall encourage, through appropriate legislation, cooperative societies. They shall also establish the means to facilitate workers' access to ownership of the means of production.' We therefore believe that this bill will fulfil its twofold objective: on the one hand, to promote the ecosystem of emerging companies in Spain, from the creation of a company to its consolidation, and, on the other hand, to stimulate investment in emerging companies and digital entrepreneurs and turn Spain into a pole of international attraction. I shall now turn to some of the main developments in this law. Start-ups will be considered those companies that are innovative new start-ups, up to five years in general or seven years in the case of biotechnology, energy or industrial companies, which have their head office, permanent establishment and the majority of employment in Spain, which have not distributed dividends and are not listed, and which have revenues of up to EUR 5 million. The procedures for its creation are facilitated, with the elimination of notarial and registered tariffs, in the case of companies that are established by means of standard statutes and by electronic means. During the first three years, startups are exempted from the cause of dissolution for related losses or resulting in a capital imbalance. The National Innovation Company S.A., ENISA, under the Ministry of Industry, Commerce and Tourism, will be responsible for accreditation as startups to be able to access the benefits established by law, and there will be points of attention to the entrepreneur and a national entrepreneurship office that will function as portals or information windows and access to public aid. It also provides for a very favourable tax regime for both companies and their investors and their workers. That is why, ladies and gentlemen, we are voting in favour. [[Applauds]]

Ladies and gentlemen, the Emerging Enterprise Ecosystem Development Act, colloquially known as the startup law, is a rule in response to the global digital reality, generated at the right time of opportunity, investment opportunity and financing. It is a milestone of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan for the attraction of talent and drive of national and international entrepreneurship. It is a law that will establish for the first time - I repeat, for the first time - for these companies a modern and specific regulatory framework; speeding up bureaucracy and eliminating formalities; promoting its creation and scalability; with a strong boost to R & D+i; promoting synergies between the various actors - the Administration, SMEs, large companies, universities, research centres and emerging companies - and generating through this innovative impulse one of the economic tractors engines to be developed today in our country. This law is a country law. Putting the existing ecosystem in Spain a little in context, we are the third European country in number of startup companies. In recent years, investment has multiplied with a major evolution, we could almost call it revolution. Regulation is therefore necessary to provide regulatory certainty at the national level and much more at the international level, to give them global visibility by enhancing their competitiveness. It is a law whose objectives are to modernise regulation, to make its procedures more flexible and to promote the ecosystem of companies by giving them more life to create startups, to be born, to grow and to be transformed. In the Socialist Parliamentary Group we believe that the fundamental premises of this rule - which are consistent with the road map of the progressive government led by Pedro Sánchez - are to promote investment and economic development and to attract talent, knowledge and innovation. An equation in which the unknown always clears itself with added values: the intergenerational value, becoming an attractive job option for our young people; the competitive value, being an international reference; the value of territorial cohesion, with telework, so that they can work where they decide to work digital nomads; the value of experience, with the novel entrepreneur in series-by-end is recognized the learning -, and finally, the value of the effort of the entrepreneur, because it is enough of heroes: dignification and facilities. The Socialist Party is once again contributing to turning Spain into a level reference in technological entrepreneurship across Europe in sectors that encompass biotechnology, energy and industry; in strategic sectors of vital importance in current geopolitics; in technologies such as artificial intelligence, blockchain, big data, robotics, quantum computing, nanotechnology and a long-term, developing world-class reference technology. We are the first European country to legislate in this respect with the search for the grail of parliamentary consensus, on the road to dialogue and with a long look. Ladies and gentlemen, you will allow me a memory. This weekend, the Socialist Party commemorated forty years of the first socialist government. So many memories of pioneering, innovative and modernizing laws at that time and in a country that demanded them invite us to reflect. It is another time, yes, but with technological needs now evidenced by the pandemic, by war or by telework, with a need for digital transformation that implies being brave at difficult times. Today we are taking a step forward in our country's progress to advance in this future of changes, also in technology, hand in hand, together, adding them to give a yes to a demanded and worked law, a pioneering law in Europe, a law to attract talent, knowledge and investment. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u44	Thank you very much, Mrs. Calvo. For the Republican Parliamentary Group, Mr Álvarez and Mr García have the floor. Mr ÁLVAREZ I GARCIA: Thank you, President. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. The moment of truth has arrived for the law of sport, a law that, as you know, has had two key moments and the two have been starred by the Republican Left. Thanks to Esquerra Republicana, we will ensure that the law ensures sustainability and the environment. Thanks to Esquerra Republicana, we will ensure that the law ensures the rights of animals in sport. ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u64	We turn to the following item: Opinion of the draft law on institutionalization of the evaluation of public policies in the General Administration of the State. To defend the amendments made to this legislative initiative by the Citizens' Parliamentary Group, Mr López-Bas Valero has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u65	Thank you, Madam President. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. Citizens have always defended as a party the need to institutionalize the evaluation of public policies. In fact, we believe that, in political action, decisions taken to serve the interests of citizens are essential to have a set of procedures and criteria that help, first, to optimize scarce resources, to give rigour to the decisions taken to use those resources and thus to be able to better detect pros and cons in the different options that can be given. Secondly, we believe that this system should also serve to analyse decisions already taken, see what real impact they have and if they achieve the objectives, in order to change course and improve what has already been taken. This is the evaluation of public policies, a diagnosis of possible needs and alternatives, or an ex ante evaluation, and then analyze the consequences of decisions made, or an ex post evaluation. [[The Lord]] In Citizens we have always maintained that these evaluation processes must be fully and naturally assumed, as they are the true connection between the democratic system and the scientific method. It is the tool that allows to design and make the best and most effective decisions to govern well, but above all with greater transparency and strengthening the accountability of those who govern to the governed. But is this the bill we want in Citizens? Well, look, to be perfectly clear, this is the first step that we think is necessary, but it is not much less the whole way forward, and in this we have been honest in the dialogue with the Socialist Group and its spokesman, Mr Aceves, whom, in any case, I have to thank for their availability and for their reciprocity. I would like to remind you, ladies and gentlemen, that Citizens already introduced in February and October 2016, in the respective agreements for possible governments of Spain both with PSOE and with the People's Party, steps towards the implementation of a real system of public policy evaluation. We did this first with the PSOE, in order to strengthen the independence and increase the resources of the extinct State Agency for the Evaluation of Public Policies, AEVAL, which was born in 2006 and disappeared in 2017 as a result of the lack of resources and the absence of a culture of evaluation in Spain, but above all because of the refusal of the incumbent politician to be evaluated and analyzed in his decisions. And so we then agreed with the People's Party to replace that agency with - I quote literally - a new independent institute for the analysis and evaluation of social policies to assess the effectiveness and cost of public spending policies and programmes, including educational, health and social policies at the national level, independently, professionally and effectively. This created the Institute for the Evaluation of Public Policies, which since 2017 has unfortunately had very limited work and, of course, without any real autonomy and independence of the Government to which it is called to evaluate. Because that is the horse of battle, the independence of the evaluating body, and there is still much to go on here. Citizens' position is to ask us, if it is to evaluate the action and decisions of the Government, why we let the Government itself somehow carry out or control that assessment. Why this Congress does not demand a truly independent evaluation agency, with its own budget, in an independent section of the general budgets of the State. Why don't we bring this agency by law to these Courts, which are the direct representation of the citizens, and not to a ministry, which is a delegated body of the government in charge. Why, ladies and gentlemen, do we all, as Members of Parliament in our role of monitoring and controlling the government, give up just as important an instrument as this evaluating agency and give it to the one to be evaluated? In short, why do we agree to risk that the evaluation of public policies will end up again being ineffective or marginal, or, worse, that it will end up being an instrument of evaluation, but of mere timely and interested justification for the action of the Government. That is why we are critical of this first step, but we are also constructive and we appreciate it, even if only because there were European funds in the middle. We are sure that it would not have been included in the plan of the government before Europe if it had not been precisely by Europe a constant and permanent claim to Spain. But it is well that this first step has been taken; a first step of many that we still believe must be taken to achieve a true evaluation of public policies in Spain that is effective, comprehensive and truly independent. For this reason, Citizens we have been the ones who have believed most in this initiative and those who have worked most actively in it. Of the one hundred amendments to the draft, thirty-six are ours and nineteen have been included directly or by way of compromise in the opinion we are debating today. That is why we are satisfied and voting yes, but at the same time we are aware that it is not the whole law of public policy evaluation that we need in Spain. We keep alive for this plenary nine of our amendments, numbers 36, 37, 48, 56, 59, 62, 67, 69 and 70. They aim to increase the administrative scope to be evaluated, because we believe that this law has, indeed, to have an integral and non-residual vocation to analyze or evaluate sectors of the General Administration of the State that no longer have a process of evaluation of their own, but that, for example, it is not established in a legal way, as it tries to do this law. And we also want every evaluation to always draw up a plan for the use of results that sets out concrete actions based on the evaluation that is carried out. We believe, on the other hand, that the new agency should already be created through this law, as indicated by Law 40/2015 itself, and not that it merely authorizes its creation, something we have not just understood. We also find it necessary to give researchers access to the results of the evaluation, precisely in order to ensure that this research, result and evaluation are more transparent and thus to achieve more effective results. We also propose a public policy experimentation unit that will make it possible to make progress through evaluation innovation. And finally, we propose a series of reforms in Law 19/2013, on transparency, access to public information and good governance; a rule that we really believe requires a review after almost a decade of validity. We want to start now and we do so through our amendments; for example, not to limit access to data from the Administration, which are of all and have often been paid for by everyone, moving away from alleged intellectual property rights by the Government; or, also, to clarify the calculation of time limits in requests for information by those administered; or, finally, to implement a sanctioning regime in a rule that sets obligations for the Administration and its officials, but that does not, however, set out what happens if they are not complied with. It is precisely in order to improve this law, ladies and gentlemen, that we call for support for the amendments tabled. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u69	With the coming of the presidency. Ladies and gentlemen, first of all, I would like to say that my parliamentary group, the VOX Group, is in favour of the evaluation of public policies, provided that its purpose is to analyse and highlight the impact of such policies on issues as crucial for citizenship as the unity of Spain and national sovereignty; territorial cohesion and equality between Spaniards; the environment and rural Spain; the protection of the socio-economically disadvantaged; depopulation and the demographic challenge; economic growth; decent, stable and quality employment; intergenerational solidarity; and the just redistribution of wealth, among others, while respecting the legal and constitutional order of our nation. [[Laughter and applause]] As I was saying, there is now an institute and, of course, that of being a director of the same institute sounded bad to him and they said: let's put state agency, which sounds nicer to be director of a state agency, because it doesn't make any sense. Where appropriate, what should be done was to provide the current institute with the necessary resources and to expand or strengthen its powers. Currently, the Institute for the Evaluation of Public Policy has the following functions, which coincide with what is contained in the draft law, but are divided into three agencies: evaluation of public policies, plans and programmes in coordination with ministerial departments; promotion of the culture of public policy evaluation; preparation, dissemination and publication of reports, technical documents and other documents of interest and methodologies and evaluation guides; training in public policy evaluation and, in particular, training of public employees in this area, in coordination with INAP; the instrumental support necessary to carry out the analyses required by the modernization or planning processes that are being promoted; and participation in agencies, organizations and forums related to the evaluation of public policies with collaborative options. Of course, it does not contain anything that this should be done with a gender perspective and based on the dogmas of the 2030 Agency, which is what you like and, then, you decide that we need to change it. On the other hand, the definition made by the current Institute for the Evaluation of Public Policies of the activity and purposes of the evaluation of public policies is also consistent with what is contained in the draft law. First, the current institute defines the evaluation of public policies as the systematic and reasoned process of knowledge generation from the collection, analysis and interpretation of information, aimed at the overall understanding of a public intervention - whether it be a policy, plan, program or norm - to achieve an evidence-based value judgement regarding its design, implementation and effects. It is literal as it comes in the bill. Secondly, with regard to the purposes of the evaluation, the institute stresses, as the draft law also does, that the evaluation promotes transparency and accountability of citizens and contributes to the improvement of democratic quality, assists in evidence-based decision-making, promotes effectiveness and efficiency in public action by promoting greater rationality of public expenditure, serves learning and contributes to the improvement of the strategic and operational dimension of public action and encourages participation in public action. In our opinion, this bill is one more example of the socialcommunist legislation, which loves to create chiringuitos, to act as a agency for the placement of friends of the government, and hence creates agency, commission and advice to place three directors general, deputy directors general, friends... We see no point in it, and that is why we have tabled a total of 30 amendments which respond to these four proposals. First, to make ex ante, intermediate and ex post evaluations mandatory for all public policies and to link them to the expenditure monitoring activity of the AIReF. Secondly, change the criteria of public value, final objectives and indicators that will determine the evaluation of public policies and define them in a closed and objective way to avoid their instrumentalization in the service of left-wing ideologies and globalist agendas. Thirdly, to abolish new equipment, units or bodies - as I said before, nothing less than three - which is covered by the bill. And fourthly, to reduce the periodicity of public policy assessment plans that are required of the Government and ministerial departments. Well, none of our 30 amendments have been accepted in the Commission, so we are keeping them alive and we are going to put them to your consideration. Mr. President, I conclude. Once again, it is shown that this Government is thinking of the welfare of politicians rather than the welfare of citizens. Nothing more and thank you very much. ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u71	President, Members, the evaluation of public policies is an essential tool to improve the implementation of government actions, to have more effective and efficient policies, as well as to be accountable to citizens. The OECD has highlighted the importance of institutionalizing the evaluation of public policies to improve the use of public resources and to make decision-making more transparent. In the same vein, the European Union has been insisting on the desirability of creating a regulatory framework covering all evaluation processes. With the governments of the People's Party, through the Commission for the Reform of Public Administrations, created in October 2012, a great boost was given to evaluation through the analysis of the situation of the public sector, improving its functioning and reducing its costs. [[CORA]] Also, through the Organic Law 6/2013, establishing the AIReF, and the Law 19/2013, on transparency, access to public information and good governance, great progress has been made in strengthening public services by safeguarding the budgetary stability of the government's economic policy and facilitating adequate public sector funding; the public sector has also been endowed with great transparency by introducing a number of accountability mechanisms to citizens. There are already a number of units within the General Administration of the State with competence for evaluation, for example, the activities of service inspections, also quality inspections, monitoring and regulatory evaluation, etc. But there is no one-size-fits-all approach to public policy evaluation. That is why, from our parliamentary group, we have set out to establish a common methodology, on a cross-cutting basis, which covers all sectors and serves the essential purpose of ensuring that government actions have a real impact on the living conditions of citizens and also on the exercise of their rights, which we believe is absolutely necessary, since we have a government whose actions are absolutely irresponsible, unthinkable and lack transparency. In summary, we consider it necessary to structure the public policy evaluation system of the General State Administration as a tool for improving public service, accountability and transparency, with the aim, as I said, of achieving effectiveness and efficiency in political action. We are convinced that the evaluation of public policies will contribute to the reflection on decision-making and to the knowledge of the impact that a given public action has on citizens; that we really see the impact that it has had on the implementation of certain public and political actions and decisions. That is why our amendments have been aimed at improving this law and giving it greater technical and legal rigour so that it can be a real tool for evaluating public policies. ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u72	Thank you. Mr Gimeno has the floor on behalf of the Joint Parliamentary Group.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u73	Good morning, Mr. President, ladies and gentlemen. We value this project as a substantial advance in public policy. The evaluation of public policies in order to optimize the use of scarce resources to achieve maximum social welfare and greater equity before choosing a measure, and also to evaluate its capacity and effectiveness a posteriori, is a traditional deficiency that we have in Spain. The lack of evaluation of the policies that have taken place in the country has surely missed the possibility of knowing the real effects of many policies designed not always on criteria of efficiency, social equity and territorial cohesion, so it is evident that in Spain the outstanding evaluation is one that focuses on the contribution of each of the policies to contribute to the territorial balance. We do not have an assessment of the extent to which development-oriented sectoral policies have been designed and implemented with this objective of balance, for example, in order to reflect and analyse the extent to which a particular policy has contributed to the fight against depopulation. It is essential to ask for the design of the following policies to be better and more efficient. As the explanatory statement points out, public policy evaluation should be an essential tool for improving government actions, as it favours informed decision-making and serves as an element for proposing possible corrections. We have made contributions to the extent of our possibilities, although we would have liked the law to contemplate more decisively and accurately the assessment from the perspective of the contribution to this territorial cohesion. Article 4 (e) of the draft law - which refers to the final objectives of the evaluation of public policies - incorporates that the evaluation of public policies will aim to contribute to improving the situation of the challenges of society and sustainable development, such as combating depopulation and territorial cohesion; equal treatment and opportunities between women and men; decent, stable and quality employment; meeting the needs of socially vulnerable groups and environmental sustainability, among others. Article 6 (g) - common technical aspects in the design of public policies - states that the following components are common technical aspects in the design of these policies: intergenerational balance; demographic challenge and territorial cohesion; digital transformation; social and environmental sustainability; and social inclusion of persons and groups in situations of social vulnerability. In any case, even if this is taken up, we believe that the implementation of the law will need to ensure that the fight against depopulation and territorial cohesion are taken up as one of the components of public policy assessment. A very important step of the law in this respect is that transactional number 8 with Teruel Existe gives rise to a new additional sixth provision whereby the Government will promote a rural guarantee mechanism, ensuring the participation of stakeholders interested in its design and implementation. This mechanism shall include, in any case, the incorporation of the assessment of the territorial and environmental effects and rural society of public policies, in accordance with the provisions of Article 2.1 of this Law, and also the development of a specific evaluation methodology taking into account the principles, recommendations and tools proposed by the European Union in this area. We would have liked more detail and detail in the drafting of this additional provision, as well as transferring the link with the rural guarantee mechanism to other articles. In any case, we want to insist on the value and meaning of this law on public policy assessment to reflect that figure which creates the rural guarantee mechanism and which must have further development. It is simply a question of applying in Spain the Rural Proofing that is being designed and implemented in European countries. You know that this is a demand that Teruel Existe has transferred to this House and it will surely be necessary to discuss and analyse the content and regulation of this rural guarantee mechanism. From our point of view, this mechanism should carry out, within the framework of the evaluation of public policies, the analysis, evaluation and correction of these policies from a territorial cohesion and combating depopulation approach, and should focus its approach in accordance with the provisions of this law and taking into account the real and potential impacts and their effects on rural areas with regard to certain aspects that are fundamental to them such as prosperity; strengthening value chains; increasing rural employment; knowledge and innovation in social and economic activities; preserving rural life; the social well-being of their communities; cultural life; improving governance; landscape care; environmental quality; territorial sustainability; biodiversity; managing natural resources; promoting climate action and caring for the natural environment. Similarly, we believe that a specific evaluation methodology should be developed taking into account the principles, recommendations and tools proposed by the European Union in this area and that it should apply the principles contained in this law. It will be necessary for this methodology for the preparation of impact reports on territorial cohesion, depopulation and rural environment to form part of the legal reports and memory of any draft law and for this methodology to progressively adapt existing laws with regard to their impact on territorial cohesion, depopulation and rural environment. It seems essential, therefore, that a real decree or similar figure shortly implements the rural guarantee mechanism and that this public policy tool be integrated into the Public Policy Evaluation Agency that creates this bill. For us, this bill is a substantial step forward in establishing for the first time the rural guarantee mechanism in Spain, which we hope will be developed urgently. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u77	Thank you, Mr. President. In our group we will vote in favour of the opinion and against all the amendments that have been kept alive. We understand that it is a good law that follows the approach that we believe is right for the implementation of the evaluation of public policies and, above all and above all, it moves away from any temptation to establish the evaluation of public policies as a kind of technocratic corset to condition the decision-making processes and the processes of implementation of those measures, programmes or laws that are passed in parliaments and are applied by democratically elected governments. The law, as has been said, responds to the requirements that have been made for years by both the OECD and the European Union for States to implement and systematize evaluation policies with the idea of improving decision-making, improving accountability and transparency and making effective and efficient use of public resources. In particular, this law fits into reform 1 of component 11 of the commitments made by the State and Government of Spain within the Transformation and Resilience Recovery Plan, and constitutes the milestone ICD 146. We believe that the objective of the law, which is dictated in its article 1, is perfectly fulfilled in the design that has been made of the text. We do not share the opinion of VOX's spokesperson; we believe that the attempt to systematize evaluation policies is fully covered, and there is nothing in that process that is outside the scope of the law. Another thing is that, being a law of a basic nature, there will still be development regulations through the corresponding regulations, but the law defines the different types of evaluation that will be carried out during the process, both in terms of its content and in terms of the time when it will be applied; it defines the process in its different stages and its various requirements; it points out who, when and how the evaluation will be carried out; it talks about the establishment of different types of indicators that will apply to such assessments; it implements a system of governance for the matter, and we believe that this is all that is needed for such a law. Another thing will be its development and application over time, but we cannot share that view that it is a law that is poorly raised from a technical point of view. Elements we like especially? On the one hand, the fact that they are going to lay the foundations for implementing the use of these indicators that will make the evaluation processes possible in a comparative way and that they are mainly aimed at the creation of a corpus of scientific knowledge that will be consulted and will be available to the different researchers and that in the long run can serve to improve the decision-making process, as mentioned above. We also like that it goes far beyond what would be the economic and budgetary assessment itself. Throughout the law, mention is made of the need to measure the impact that public policies can have on issues such as gender equality; the environment; the energy transition; the extension of social rights; depopulation; the demographic challenge; economic growth; decent, stable and quality employment; intergenerational solidarity; redistribution of wealth, etc. We believe that this is the right approach to evaluation, which must be the way in which the impact of public policies on the lives of societies is measured, on the objectives to be determined, and we like to see it move away from that other framework that is established mainly through the control of the budgetary and fiscal impacts that policies have, as has been customary so far. Having said that, we believe that this is not a baladi issue, but a key element. Fedea's criticism of the law is that it is a disappointing, unambitious and confusing text, and is based primarily on her understanding that there is no real independence from the work of the Government. We understand that the role of evaluation is, indeed, to confirm that policies meet the objectives proposed, but taking into account all these social and rights issues and to contribute to the development of society from the point of view of equity and the components that make societies progress beyond the budgetary aspects. When approaches are made to create, for example, an independent authority that converts evaluation into a kind of IREF of evaluation and, as the spokesman for the VOX Group has openly said, that it could even function in a coordinated manner with IREF so that evaluation policies are determined by the rigour of budgetary issues and all this is incorporated into the decision-making process with that perspective, we understand that it would be a serious attack on the functioning of democracy and that it should be very vigilant for this never to happen. This law does not happen, fortunately, but I think it is clear that there are groups that will do their utmost to make this happen in the future. You'll have to be alert so this doesn't happen. For this reason, we keep alive the amendment to Article 5, the information system for farms and livestock and for agricultural production, known as SIEX, because we are concerned that no transitional formulas are allowed to this tool, especially for those who may need it for various reasons. ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u111	Thank you, President. Today the parliamentary procedure in the Congress of the draft law on the CAP is complete. However, we have adopted four amendments, we have negotiated three more and today we will vote on the remaining ten amendments for which we call for the support of the groups. I would particularly like to highlight the incorporation in the Paper, during the sitting held on 5 October, of our Amendment No 85, which was incorporated into the text of the Paper by a majority of the parliamentary groups and without any vote against. This amendment is essential for the primary sector by lifting the ban on burning plant waste in the agricultural environment. Article 27(3) of Law 7/2022 of 8 April on contaminated waste and soil for a circular economy, in force since last April, is deleted. The ban on burning plant waste is a clear example of disconnection between rural needs and those who sometimes act from their backs to the needs of the sector. The obligation to dispose of these materials through certain processes provided for in the Waste Law obliges agricultural holdings to make costly investments which, in the current drought situation, the increase in inputs and the international situation, are not in a position to cope. It should not be forgotten that the precept directly affects more than 900,000 agricultural holdings, of the order of 12 million hectares of arable areas of herbaceous crops and almost 5 million hectares of permanent and woody crops. For this reason and because the burning of plant waste generated in the agricultural or forestry environment is a management practice of farms subject to strict rules sufficiently regulated by the autonomous Community legislation on environmental effects and by fire prevention regulations, it is appropriate to have deleted it from the law on contaminated waste and soil.

This is the only way to focus aid for farming itself. That has not been done and we believe that, in that sense, that effort to redistribute has not been made. The proposal of the European Commission for the CAP incorporates the new figure which is laid down in the strategic plans drawn up by each Member State. As with other raw materials and critical materials, some basic elements of fertilizers for agriculture are beginning to run out. ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u131	Thank you. Mr Pagès i Massó has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u132	Thank you, President. On 10 May you, ladies and gentlemen of the PSOE, presented the proposal for a law to reform the Criminal Code, which today will complete its legislative procedure in this House. At that time, Congresswoman Mariona Illamola, whom I replaced today, already demonstrated - as most political parties did - the discrimination that his proposal entailed, because it only aggravated the penalty for traffickers if the victims came from the war conflict motivated by the invasion of the Ukrainian territory by Russia, and this only for a period of eighteen months. We're not there anymore. The text has improved, but from our point of view it still presents a problem. As a result of the accepted amendments, both the title and the explanatory statement stipulate that the penalties for crimes of trafficking in human beings displaced by armed conflict or humanitarian catastrophe should be increased. This text emphasizes the category of the person, displaced by armed conflict or humanitarian catastrophe, displaced person who may be trafficked at any time. Their vulnerability is present at all times because of conflict or catastrophe. They are vulnerable because they are displaced. However, the wording which now comes to us from Article 167a(4) provides that the victim is a person whose vulnerability has been caused or aggravated by displacement resulting from an armed conflict or humanitarian catastrophe. The different wording emphasizes displacement rather than the category of the displaced person, which goes into the background. The penalty is not aggravated because it is vulnerable to displacement, but because it is captured during displacement. Therefore, if it is not captured during displacement, the penalty will not be increased. I repeat, if the person is captured before or after displacement, the aggravating would not apply, according to the literality. Examples: first, a person flees from a country where there is an armed conflict - Ukraine - and arrives in Spain hosted by a family or via temporary protection. It arrives without any problem, but once in Spanish territory, due to its precariousness, it is captured by a network of trafficking. According to the text adopted, the aggravating factor would not apply, since its vulnerability would have occurred in Spain. Secondly, a person flees from a country where there is an armed conflict-Ukraine - and on his journey to Spain is captured by a trafficking network. According to the text adopted, the aggravating factor would apply to it since its vulnerability has been aggravated by displacement. Ms Illamola, defending the position of several academics on this issue, has argued that there is a disparity between the title and the explanatory statement with the article resulting from the parliamentary procedure, the literality of the article being more limited in scope, which may lead to confusion in its interpretation in the court. In order for all cases of vulnerability to be collected, Junts submitted a compromise proposal in the Paper and in the Commission which was not accepted. However, we believe that our amendment, which we have kept alive, better reflects the spirit of the reform that is being proposed: aggravating penalties for people who traffic in human beings displaced by armed conflict or humanitarian catastrophe, people whose vulnerability can be given at any time - we have just seen it - by armed conflict or humanitarian catastrophe. If our proposal, our amendment, is not accepted, we will also vote in favour of the text, as we believe it is an improvement on existing legislation. However, we believe that this is, unfortunately, a partial improvement. Thank you very much.

As has already been said, there have been some other amendments pending for discussion within the broader framework, as has also been pointed out, of a future and pending general law on trafficking, such as not limiting the aggravation to capture only during displacement, but extending it at any time, provided that the situation of the person caught by trafficking networks can be connected to the flight of a war conflict or a humanitarian catastrophe; although, adversely, this reform sought to place the singular emphasis on the confusion that generates massive and disorderly exoduses, which is the context for trafficking networks, mafias, to spread their tentacles with deception to capture women, children and minors for exploitation or sale, and within the exploitation of their sexual abuse or exploitation. ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u141	Thank you very much. Mrs Velarde Gómez has the floor for the Confederal Parliamentary Group of the United Nations Podemos-En Comú Podem-Galicia in common.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u142	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, our parliamentary group has tabled amendments with criteria based on the considerations made by the Ministry of Equality, and there are other parliamentary groups that have tabled amendments in the same direction, and the original text presented by the Socialist Group in this House has therefore been amended. In the end, this amendment to the Criminal Code is not confined to the war conflict in Ukraine, nor is it a temporary, but a definitive measure. With this amendment of the Criminal Code, which we are going to adopt today, the penalties are appropriately increased and a new aggravated modality is introduced in article 177 bis, paragraph 4, of the Organic Law 10/1995 of 23 November, of the Criminal Code, if the victim of trafficking is a person whose vulnerability has been caused or, where appropriate, aggravated by displacement resulting from an armed conflict or a humanitarian catastrophe. I would like to recall that, in accordance with the international commitments made by our country, our State has and must respond to trafficking in persons with due diligence, as stated by the United Nations rapporteur on trafficking in persons, especially women and children, in four key areas with an institutional response. The first, prevention. The second is the obligation to identify, assist and support victims. The third, criminalization, investigation, prosecution and punishment, hence the increase in the penalties for the greater vulnerability of the victims. And fourth - of course - cooperation between interstate institutions. In the same vein, the report of 21 February 2021 of the Committee on Civil Liberties, Justice and Home Affairs and the Committee on Women's Rights and Gender Equality of the European Parliament on the implementation of Directive 2011/36 of the European Union on the prevention and combating of trafficking in human beings and the protection of victims is also delivered. The document urged States to establish severe criminal sanctions for trafficking offences. In addition, reference is also made to measures to improve the identification and care of victims, including some related to migration and asylum. The report also affirms the need to redouble efforts to combat impunity, bringing to justice those who exploit and abuse victims. That fight against impunity, precisely that fight, is one of the fundamental objectives of both the Ministry of Equality and, in this case, the Ministry of Justice. This report of the Special Rapporteur points out that the obligation of the State is fundamental to identifying many other aspects of the State's own obligations. And it specifically cites the investigation, prosecution of traffickers and protection of trafficked persons. All this will have to be discussed more broadly - because that is not what we are dealing with today, ladies and gentlemen of the People's Party - in the next law on trafficking. [[Rumors]]

ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u305	Ladies and gentlemen, I should like to inform you that from 16 hours until 17 hours and 30 minutes today the rest of the items on the agenda which will be put to the vote in the Chamber at the end of the sitting will be voted on by telematics. I should also like to inform you that this plenary session includes the debate and adoption of the opinion of the Proposed Organic Law amending the Organic Law 10/1995 of 23 November of the Criminal Code, in order to aggravate the penalties provided for crimes of trafficking in human beings displaced by armed conflict or a humanitarian catastrophe. In view of its organic nature, this initiative must be put to a final vote which, in accordance with Rule 131.2 of the Rules of Procedure, must be announced in advance by the Presidency. Accordingly, pursuant to this provision, he announced that such votes would not take place before seventeen and thirty minutes.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u306	We continue with the point corresponding to the validation or repeal of the Royal Decree Law 18/2022 of 18 October, adopting measures to strengthen the protection of consumers of energy and to contribute to the reduction of the consumption of natural gas in application of the Plan + safety for your energy, as well as measures regarding the remuneration of staff in the service of the public sector and the protection of potential agricultural workers affected by the drought. To present the royal decree law, I have the floor, on behalf of the Government, the third Vice-President and Minister for the Ecological Transition and the Demographic Challenge.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u307	Thank you very much, President. Very good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen, and thank you very much for being in this session, in which I intend to ask you for the validation of Decree Law 18/2022, approved, as indicated by the President, last October 18 in the Council of Ministers. It is a decree law incorporating some measures integrated into the Energy Security Plan, particularly those that we believe have a particularly direct connection with consumer protection, both domestic and industrial. And I do so one day immediately after I have been able to present and discuss in the Committee on Ecological Transition the detailed details of this plan, its objectives, the fundamental axes, the analysis of what is happening in Europe, always with caution, with prudence, knowing that Spain is in a greater security position than that of our neighbours and also knowing that we must not lower the guard, that probably the need to pay particular attention to winter 2022-2023 will be added to those of successive winters. [[The lady]] I would also like to say here in this House that, as I shared yesterday with the Members participating in the Commission, not only did I fulfil my commitment to come forward with the plan, but I would like to have the opportunity to continue to do so, at least once a quarter, because we need to strengthen energy security... [[Rumors]]
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u308	Quiet, ladies and gentlemen, please.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u309	... because we need, in this context of energy security, the Government to keep its particular attention on the citizens and the productive fabric of our country. We can't lower the guard, as I said before. We must continue to drive the savings, efficiency and structural response to this crisis and, in any case, we must remain particularly attentive to the social and economic impacts of a situation like this. It is not the first to live in this legislature and, as in the previous crisis, the Government has wanted to pay particular attention to the economy and to society. In the previous crisis we protected companies and workers with measures such as ERTE, ICO or self-help. We made a wall of social restraint to prevent the most vulnerable from suffering the ravages of the pandemic. We are looking for ways to achieve a rapid recovery of the country, relying on European solidarity. Today the context presents us an opportunity to continue working in this direction, in this case showing our solidarity with the Europeans, but also working on those measures that, as I said before, are in a position to support the citizens and the productive fabric in Spain in a situation of high energy prices that ends up impacting on inflation. We do not want the social and economic conditions of the Spaniards to be affected by this situation. We believe that we must pay particular attention to the vulnerable. In this sense, the tax and reduction of regulated costs that we have been adopting for months has made it possible to compensate by 39% the increase in energy costs on the final electricity bill between the first half of 2021 and the first half of 2022. In the Spanish retail market, where there is a high percentage of the regulated tariff, known as the PVPC, approximately 40%, Spain has a third position when assessing the highest increase in the cost of the invoice before taxes; however, if we take into account the compensation that has been experienced with the tax effort and the reduction of the regulated costs, we are placed in the 14th position of the European Union in the first half of 2022 in relation to the first half of 2021, that is, before the application of the Iberian mechanism, whose operation began last 15 June, was effective. In total, the effort accumulated since June 2021, when we began to take measures to protect families, to date amounts to EUR 35 billion, i.e. almost 3% of the GDP in Spain dedicated to preserving the economy and the well-being of workers, families, businesses and the self-employed, with the conviction that living a crisis does not mean giving up fighting for equity, fighting for progress, because we are convinced that we do not want to get out of it impoverished or with a devastated productive fabric; on the contrary, we want to ensure a fair response, a rapid capacity for recovery in the way out and a orientation at times of change that allows for the modernization and dynamism of our economy. The preparation of the Energy Security Plan has offered us a magnificent opportunity to listen, to dialogue with all actors, and, I repeat, I believe it is important to maintain and enrich this dynamic. We want to work together, build consensus as reflected in the plan, and we want to do so with all the forces of the parliamentary bow. This Decree-Law includes fundamental measures for the enhanced social protection of which he spoke. First, we extend the limit to the growth of the regulated rate of natural gas for families and SMEs until 31 December 2023; therefore, a limitation to the increase in the rate that has been applied since June 2021 and will extend - as I said - until the end of 2023. In addition, this standard introduces a new tariff that will benefit community owners with community heating systems. Because we want to support families and facilitate the behaviour of responsible energy consumption, we have designed a new tariff, in force also until 31 December 2023, to which all the communities of owners can be welcomed - we estimate that about 1.7 million households - provided that two conditions are met. The first is that individualized counter systems must be installed by September 2023. The commitment to do so by the community is sufficient to access the fee. Incorporating these meters before the end of September facilitates the gradual installation and access to the item of 40 million euros to replace analog meters that will make this improvement in knowledge of our consumption easier and more affordable. The second condition, besides having updated the review of boilers, thus ensuring their proper functioning, is to maintain the incentive to efficiency and savings in this type of consumption. We therefore calculate the average consumption of the community over the past five years, and 70% of that consumption is subject to a 40% discount, which means that the communities of owners who maintain the consumption will benefit from a reduction in the costs of their bill, which will reach about 50% savings in relation to an invoice that does not benefit from this tariff. Another important development is the creation of an extraordinary and temporary fee for families with up to 28 000 euros of income, with a number of people living in the same household, who, depending on the number of people and the characteristics of these people - minors or any other type of condition that allows access to the maximum of the reduction - can access a very significant reduction in the bonus, that is, what they pay in their electricity consumption and in their gas consumption. The neighbouring rate and the exceptional rate for families with incomes of up to 28 000 euros are new protection measures that extend the coverage of the middle class, of the working classes, but, at the same time, it is essential to strengthen the protection instruments intended for vulnerable consumers who today benefit from the electric social bonus. For this reason, we have increased the coverage of the electric social bond with up to 65 and 80% discount for vulnerable and severe, respectively; we have increased the allocation in the general budgets of the State to finance the thermal social bond both in 2022 and in 2023; we have increased the minimum amount of the aid, from the minimum 25 euros in the thermal social bond to the 40 euros monthly, and the average aid is raised to 375 euros for heating per beneficiary, and we have expanded the electricity consumption covered by the social bond. Let me also remind you of the obvious, which is also answered in this decree law. A situation like this requires maximum transparency, the best rigour in consumer information on their invoices. This is why, in accordance with the requests of the main consumer and user organisations, our invoice, starting in December, must incorporate the average consumption of households of the same postal code with a contracted power equivalent to ours; it must facilitate access to the information in a similar way to the comparison of offers that exists in the National Commission on Markets and Competition; it must introduce information approved both with regard to the tax reductions that we all benefit from, and in those cases where companies transfer the cost of gas adjustment, and linked to the Iberian mechanism, the merchants must reflect on the invoices the average cost of that adjustment in the last natural month in order to facilitate comparison, proportionality, between energy consumed and adjustment applied by the trader. Industrial consumers maintain until 31 December 2023 the special possibility that allows the industry to change the terms of their natural gas contracts, reinforcing flexibility at a time when it is particularly important. Being the most relevant social support package of this decree law, the protagonist, is not the only one. The Decree-Law also includes measures in line with the widespread demand for public administrations to take on a special role in energy saving. In particular, this Decree Law regulates one of the aspects related to consumption - responsibility of the administrations - which is particularly significant, that relating to outdoor lighting. In Spain there are more than eight million points of outdoor light, of which only 30% have LED lamps. For this reason, we have adopted a technical instruction that allows us to increase the efficiency in lighting. We calculated that the change in bulbs would allow a reduction of between 39 and 67 % in the consumption of each point. In principle, this applies to outdoor lighting, roads, public-owned streets and, possibly, concessionaires of motorways or other private facilities in a management context contracted by the Administration. It is mandatory for new lights to start operation from 1 January, but it is also a sign, it is a clear orientation for those who want to replace or modernise existing installations, which also have a special regime and support linked to the recovery plan. This measure is key, as the municipal outdoor lighting consumes around 5200 gigawatts/hour per year, i.e. 2 % of the national electricity consumption. We believe that a reform of these characteristics, provided that it is applied in a general way, could lead to a reduction of the order of 65% for a service that provides exactly the same quality conditions as the current one. Another area covered by the Decree-Law has been tax incentives to boost energy efficiency and savings. The text incorporates extended tax deductions for investments in rehabilitation, allowing families to deduct up to 20% of the investment, with a maximum of EUR 5000 per year, or even, if the house has an energy rating A or B after the intervention, it can be raised to 40%, up to a maximum of EUR 7500 per year. Companies also have additional flexibility that we consider important and that can encourage self-consumption investment. The freedom of amortisation of investments in renewables for self-consumption of any facility intended for industrial, commercial and service uses is established. It is a request that has been made to us by many of the industrial and commercial associations we have been working with over the past few months, and we believe that flexibility is allowed that can affect a good number of projects, up to a maximum of EUR 500 000 in investment. That is why we want to facilitate everyone's contribution to energy efficiency and saving, something that all the participants in this consensus debate have called for and worked with the various actors in recent months: how to channel, how to facilitate and how to encourage the contributions of those who want to do so. It is obviously a contribution to the overall capacity to achieve lower consumption, but it also directly reduces the energy bill of those who invest in efficiency. Finally, together with these consumer protection measures and increased energy savings, we have introduced a number of measures to continue the drive to the energy transition with proposals for self-consumption and renewable energy. Self-consumption is a reality very positively perceived by citizens, as evidenced by the spectacular increase in capacity installed in recent years. It is a useful and effective way to reduce our bill, and it has been essential to change the regulation to make it simpler and allocate resources from the recovery plan to facilitate access to the first self-consumption investment, but it has also allowed a spectacular increase. There is one fact that we have given on some occasions and that I would like to repeat once again, which shows the commitment, the support, but also the moment that is lived in the sector and the demand on the part of the citizens, and that is that in the ten months of 2022, from January to 31 October, the equivalent of ten times the power of self-consumption that existed in June 2018 has been installed in Spain. But this enormous development that self-consumption is experiencing in Spain requires constantly updated information, on a permanent basis, to avoid putting the system at risk. Therefore, the Decree-Law also introduces the duty of network operators to communicate to the operator of the electrical system, that is to say to the Electric Network, all the installations that are connected, in order to ensure good operation, to allow the identification of the new installations and to be able to facilitate the management with full security by the operator of the system. One of the most important keys to self-consumption must be that which occurs near the place where it is consumed. As we have been learning from the development of these capabilities, from limitations, from proximity, we have wanted to increase the distance to which shared self-consumption can be installed. The decree law increases from 500 to 1000 meters the authorized distance for shared self-consumption facilities, a distance that different actors, social groups, professionals and many of you in the parliamentary arc have asked us to increase up to 2 kilometers, which, we believe, can be done by fulfilling this requirement of communication, and that I hope can be transferred through the amendments in process to the rules that you are discussing at this time. Finally, the Decree-Law includes some additional measures, such as 1.5% compensation for salaries of public employees. Let me take this measure in the selection of those I explained when I presented the decree law to validation because it is particularly important and the result of consensus. It is a salary increase agreed within the framework of the General Bureau for the Negotiation of the Civil Service which is retroactive and which will be consolidated in this year, and this increase is therefore added to the 2% increase in public salaries which entered into force on 1 January this year. In short, the overall increase for officials in 2022 will be 3.5%. She concluded, Madam President, by pointing out once again that, despite the fact that we are going through circumstances which have created strong tensions in the global and European energy markets and which have forced us over the last few months to take urgent, extraordinary, agile measures and to remain pending as far as possible in the coming months in case we need to increase or facilitate additional measures, it is important to be able to have the greatest consensus, with the greatest support for the protection of citizens and our productive fabric. That is why, again, I would like to reiterate to you the Government's request to validate this royal decree law. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
