ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u315	Thank you, President. Today we will vote in favour of the validation of Decree Law 18, which contains eighteen of the seventy-three measures of the energy security plan, specifically those aimed at mitigating the effects of the energy crisis caused by the Ukraine war on businesses and citizens in the short term, as well as others aimed at accelerating the energy transition. In addition to this, and as is customary, some measures are also adopted in this decree which have nothing to do with the war in Ukraine, such as the approval of the additional 1.5 increase for civil servants and the reduction of thirty-five to ten days so that potential workers can benefit from unemployment benefit and agricultural income in Extremadura and Andalusia, for a reduction in production due to rainfall. Among the measures, the application of a 40% discount on the electricity bill for low-income families and workers affected by the crisis and the - persistent - increase in the social bonus discounts, so that for vulnerable consumers it goes from 60 to 65 %, while for severe vulnerable consumers it goes from 70 to 80. This decree also contains a measure that this group and another series of groups requested, through a GNP and also an amendment to Decree 14: we asked to give a solution to the communities of neighbors with gas boilers, so that they could receive the existing TUR corresponding to them according to their average annual consumption per dwelling. However, in this decree, the Government has created a special TUR for communities based on the level of annual consumption but of the entire community of owners. We believe that they are two different concepts but, ultimately, the result is that they are in TUR and not in the free market. However, the decree states that in the new TUR tariff only 70% of it is subsidized, and given the current circumstances for many consumers I believe that perhaps a larger amount, even one hundred percent, could be subsidised, as we have requested, since it is a temporary measure that lasts until 2023, when moreover so far they have not been able to benefit from this tariff. We also ask that energy service companies that provide services to the communities of owners be able to benefit from TUR, and the truth is that it also collects it. However, given the problems they have in moving to this new rate, it may be advisable to establish some automatic mechanism for transferring customers to TUR; otherwise, customers will be delayed in recognising the new rates this winter and companies will be created a bottleneck. In addition, it should be recalled that the third additional provision requires that communities of owners must request prior authorization from the Directorate-General for Energy Policy and Mines through electronic registration, which may also delay these changes. We also ask to promote, implement and improve or finance the replacement of the 100 per cent of the currently deployed park of smart natural gas meters to encourage more active participation of consumers and to enable them to use available information to save on their consumption. This measure is also included, but leaves its development to a later regulation. In this sense, it might be interesting for the IDAE, with energy efficiency funds, to subsidise the implementation costs of these meters in a percentage, to give soft credits or to establish a reasonable rent on gas tariffs, because the cost of installing these meters for a family can also be high. We also stress that until December 2023 companies and self-employed persons will be able to change their contracted power more than once within a period of less than the usual twelve months, until December 2023, and also the flexibility of the extended industrial gas contracts. The mechanism for reducing the excessive remuneration of the electricity market, introduced by Decree 17/2021, is extended until 31 December so that the excess remuneration of the electricity companies can return to the system and can be directed to help self-employed people, businesses and households; in line with what Europe has established in the regulation adopted on an emergency intervention to deal with high energy prices. It is a decree, and it must be recognized, which has also given impetus to self-consumption and energy communities. Mr Matute commented on the restrictions on limits. It is that collective self-consumption does have limits, but energy communities do not. We believe, in this respect, that the energy communities would have to be regulated even more as a result of the existing directive. In this sense, we must also say that we still do not solve the lack of capacity at the points of connection to the network because, as is often said, they are copped by the big ones. There are knots that sometimes appear and disappear and you can't be waiting more than a year for the contests to come out for your award. In this sense, apart from the modifications made in relation to the access and connection to the network in this royal decree - some of them further restrict access to the networks, such as the fact that they remain reserved for competition -, regardless of whether the reserved capacity has been reduced below the limit of 100 megawatts, we believe quite the contrary that it is necessary to raise that limit of 100 megawatts so that, in accordance with the existing rules, the general criterion of managing the granting of access and connection permits is met to some extent so that the priority is temporary, since with that limit the entire capacity of the transport network has been reserved for the system of competitions, which also leads to a total blockage in access and connection to the distribution networks as required, in accordance with the existing rules for installations of more than 4 megawatts, the report of acceptability of the transmission system operator. In addition, it should be noted that such a limitation entails a blockade of new entrants of renewables in Spain, especially if there is an urgent need for the construction and development of additional projects that allow for faster progress towards the decarbonization of the economy and energy independence, even more so after the crisis of the war in Ukraine. Even if the limit is raised to 350 megas for example, more than 76,600 megawatts will remain reserved for competitions, which we understand is more than enough. However, we believe that these are sufficient measures and that they come from the energy security plan. Once again we will support them, but we understand that perhaps some could be improved a posteriori. Nothing more and thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u318	Thank you very much, Mrs. Martínez. For the Plural Parliamentary Group, I have the floor, first of all, Mr Rego Candamil.

ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-03-CD221103.u352	Thank you, President. Today we are discussing the amendment to the totality presented by the Citizens' Group to the charges on large energy companies and large financial institutions, because they are the two sectors that are benefiting most from the current economic situation, and whose consideration was accepted by this House. I think we need to remember a number of things, and my group has already said this: we cannot avoid in this context that the energy companies are obtaining extraordinary benefits that they did not expect and that are actually obtained by a certain juncture, which is nothing other than the energy shock that we are experiencing. This has also been corroborated by the CNMC. These margins, in addition, end up being transferred to families and businesses, whose situation is complicated so that they can cope with their day-to-day lives. Mrs Vehi told us today about an income pact, but the reality is that it does not exist. We are therefore faced with charges that are supported by Europe. First, in March, when it said that Member States could consider temporary tax measures on the fallen benefits of the sky and, exceptionally, take part of those returns to distribute them among consumers, and now, more recently in the case of energy, with a clear approach to this as set out in Council Regulation 2022/1854 of 6 October 2022 on emergency intervention to deal with high energy prices, in which it regulates how this tax should be. Moreover, it must be borne in mind that there are a number of European countries - the United States is already announcing it - which have already begun to adapt their tax systems to this new reality in which there are extraordinary gains in different sectors, specifically in the field of energy. Some countries are also applying taxes to banks. In my group - and I have already said this - we are not going to question the substance of both charges, they are charges well received by society and that, moreover, we all perceive as fair, because we believe that it is necessary to divide economic efforts in order to respond to the consequences of war, so that the efforts do not fall solely on citizens, let alone on the most vulnerable groups, because the crises affect us all but not all and we all suffer them equally. They are charges that seek to help families and SMEs, who really need it, and that will help to alleviate the cost they are suffering, to finance relief in solidarity and to finance public policies to deal with the consequences of the war in Ukraine. Having said that, when we discussed the Government's proposal, we first questioned the way in which this benefit was developed and also certain technical problems which we said should be solved, where appropriate, in the parliamentary procedure for legal certainty, to try to ensure that this type of measure ends up being annulled by the courts and can lead to major financial returns which, ultimately, could end up paying the society, the citizenship and the economic fabric that we seek to protect, and for the Government's proposal to be aligned at the time with what was established by Europe, simply because this tax was adopted earlier here. In this sense, the amendment to the whole presented by Citizens seeks to replace that proposed law promoted by PSOE and Unidad Podemos and whose consideration, as I have said, was endorsed by this House. This proposal, in fact, is more similar to the one proposed by Europe to draw extraordinary energy benefits and replicates the same approach to banking, but leaves electricians out on the grounds that they are already taxed by the lower income from the gas surcharge and the gas cap, which also includes Europe in its regulation. However, the European regulation makes it possible to maintain or introduce measures that further limit the market revenue of electricity producers. This proposal, unlike that of the Government, imposes extraordinary benefits which exceed by 20% the average obtained in the previous three years, but in a single year rather than two years, and ultimately by limiting their collection to consumer support measures, energy-dependent industries or subject to investment in renewables, efficiency and decarbonisation, as well as to measures to reduce consumption. This is something that is establishing Europe. In the case of the tax on credit institutions, it replicates that established for energy and suggests that the collection should be aimed at supporting bank debtors and a deduction in the IRPF in order to deduct up to EUR 400 from the payment of interest on variable-rate mortgages on habitual housing. There are two proposals, that of the Government and that which today presents Citizens, very different and which, however, we both disagree with. Indeed, you are talking about extraordinary profits, which are not about turnover, and we can share this because in this way it can be possible to assess the economic activity of companies, but not so much in the formula they propose regarding the reference period, which is more narrow than the European one and which does not take into account the existence of regulated or liberalised activities, among others. [[The Lord]] On the other hand, as far as the tax on credit institutions is concerned, the fact is that they also replicate what is said for energy, not being limited by revenue and applying to all banking institutions - in what we might perhaps agree -. However, as I have said, we are being offered a finalist collection to help bank debtors and a deduction in the IRPF of up to EUR 400 from the payment of interest on variable mortgages on habitual housing, a measure that was already rejected in this House because it did not take into account the level of income of families, because it benefited mainly those variable mortgages of the latest constitution - in which it is paid more for interest rates than capital - because it did not help those debtors who have agreed on a fixed rate may also have difficulty paying because of the rise in energy prices, food, unemployment, etc., and because the Government is also working in this direction with banking entities to establish those measures that are more appropriate. In short, they bring us an alternative to the one proposed by the Government, this time in the form of taxes, which we share, but with a shade, and which is more similar to that established by Europe, but which, in our view, must also be modified or improved. Moreover, here is the hue as to the form proposed as taxes, since it raises some charges also without contemplating - I am not surprised - the existence of the Basque and Navarra foral estates, which, as I said, I am not surprised even though you say respect the concert, that not the quota, because it is the concert and the Navarro agreement that precisely establish that when a reform of the tax legal order of the State takes place, both administrations will proceed to the relevant adaptation of the economic concert. As I have commented, our group believes that economic efforts need to be shared in order to respond to the economic consequences of the energy crisis, so that efforts do not fall solely on citizens, let alone on vulnerable groups, through this mandatory, temporary and extraordinary contribution. There is already a proposal on this, and we will therefore be looking at it in order to put forward amendments, as regards design and its articulation, with measure. Nothing more and thank you very much.

On the other hand, if we incorporate in the calculation of the taxable base the cost structure, as you propose, we would be opening the door to that tax engineering that has done so much damage to the Spanish public finances in general and the collection of corporation tax in particular.
